# Figma Decision Register

Phase 2 of the Figma coverage audit. Collects every recorded product decision
that bears on whether a Figma frame is still authoritative, so Phase 3 can tell
**"Figma is outdated"** apart from **"the code is wrong"**.

Consolidated from existing docs only. No new decisions are made here.

- **Primary source:** `ui_visual_qa.md` (D1–D64, deviation tables, "Remaining
  Figma deviations", "Still open").
- **Also:** `journal_audit.md` (D52–D57), `ui_implementation_plan.md` §6 (numbered
  #1–#12), `settings_mapping.md`, `UI_IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`.

## How Phase 3 uses this

| Kind | Meaning | Effect on Figma coverage label |
|---|---|---|
| **OVERRIDE** | Approved decision: runtime intentionally differs from Figma | Frame/element → **FIGMA OUTDATED / CONFLICTS** (cite the ID) |
| **GAP** | Figma is right; data, art or a system is missing in the game | Does **not** make Figma outdated. Coverage stays MATCH/PARTIAL. |
| **OPEN** | No decision yet | Provisional label + entry in *Questions for Sapega* |
| **TECH** | Implementation limit (engine, asset), not a product call | Does not affect the label |

## 5. Open — none in this register

Implementation-level questions from the Runtime UI Completion plan live in
`questions_for_sapega.md`. No product question in this register is open. D18 and D56 are deferred (see §4). Their audits are kept
below as reference for when the features are picked up.

### D18 — Status character grid (audit 2026-09-27, reference — deferred)

- **Figma has no character grid.** `menu-status` `58:719` shows a single-character
  header. The kit component `UI/CharacterSelector` `222:4786` (one character card: avatar,
  name, class · level, HP/SP) is **not placed in any frame**. `UI/Party Status Panel`
  `68:1177` shows a fixed list of 4 cards and has no selection state.
- **The 8-slot grid was runtime-only.** It existed in the original
  `stats_component.gd` (`CharacterButton1..8` → `_on_character_selected("player_N")`,
  plus Str/Int/Dex/Con "+" buttons) and was removed in `515bb4f3`.
- **Runtime today:** Status shows the active character only
  (`game_manager.get_active_character()`); the party panel is display-only (no input,
  no signals, max 4 cards). The active character is hard-coded to `player_1`
  (`CharacterManager.gd:14, :56`). `switch_character()` exists (`CharacterManager.gd:167`)
  but **nothing calls it**.
- **Data:** 8 characters are hard-coded defaults (`CharacterManager.gd:66-149`), with
  per-character attributes, class and equipment. No roster file, no join/leave, no party
  size concept. Level/XP are global, not per character. **No stat-point pool and no spend
  API** (deliberately excluded, `GameManager.gd:310-314`).
- **Decided (D18):** switching and Stat Points are future features; roster size undecided.

### D56 — dormant quest engine (audit 2026-09-27, reference — deferred)

- **What exists:** 5 scripts in `SampleProject/Scripts/Quest/` (615 lines, plus 5 `.gd.uid`):
  `SceneQuestManager` (307, linear per-scene stages advanced by finished dialogues),
  `SceneQuestConfig` (61), `QuestStageResource` (56), `QuestProgressUI` (152),
  `QuestDialogueItem` (39). **No authored data** (`.tres`/`.tscn`/`.json`). Unrelated to the
  `DialogueQuest` dialogue addon.
- **Use:** never instanced — not an autoload, not in any scene, not loaded, not registered.
  Only `Canyon.gd:30` and `Village.gd:31` do a `get_node_or_null("SceneQuestManager")` that
  always returns null.
- **Cost of keeping it:** negligible at runtime (two dead lookups plus two info logs); no
  startup parsing, no `_process`, no tests. Maintenance cost: 5 global `class_name`s and
  dead guarded code in Canyon/Village. Latent bugs: `Engine.has_singleton("EventBus")`
  (:47) would stop it hearing dialogues; `_restore_progress` is an empty TODO, so progress is
  never saved.
- **Journal today:** `journal_component.gd` uses only `Game.objective_updated` /
  `current_objective`. Its `set_entries()` (id, title, state, objectives, description) is
  never called.
- **To use it for the Journal later:** instance a manager per scene; author `.tres`
  configs; fix the EventBus check; add save/restore; map stages → entries; add
  player-facing objective text (stages hold raw dialogue IDs); solve the cross-scene log,
  because each manager covers only one scene.
- **Deletion list:** the 5 `.gd` + 5 `.gd.uid`; `Canyon.gd:30, :73, :638-673`;
  `Village.gd:31, :41, :255-290` (lines 30/31 use the type and would break compilation if
  left); stale comment `journal_component.gd:9-10`; docs
  `docs/QUEST_SYSTEM_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/QUEST_SYSTEM_SETUP.md`,
  `docs/QUEST_SYSTEM_SUMMARY.md`, `design/journal_audit.md:106`, `design/ui_visual_qa.md:1045`.
  No scenes or tests break.
- **Decided (D56):** keep dormant; revisit when quest implementation begins.

`ui_implementation_plan.md` §6 still lists #2, #3, #7, #8, #9 and #12 as **Open**, but
later phases settled them: Equipment was composed from `258:5110` + `258:5111`
(#2), Skills got a data model (#3), #7 became D42, the HUD was built from

## 7. No decisions exist yet for

None of the 28 Figma frames that `figma_inventory.md` lists as never mapped has any
recorded decision: Splash, Main Menu, Save/Load, Modals & Popups, Dialogues, NPC
Interaction, Shop sell/confirm, Crafting, Enchant, Tutorial, Healing (beyond D6).
In Phase 3, any Figma-vs-runtime difference on these surfaces is **OPEN** by
default and goes to Sapega. It is never assumed to be an override.

## 8. Sapega's answers to Q1–Q28 — 2026-09-29

All open questions in [[questions_for_sapega]] answered in one pass. Recorded
verbatim in intent; nothing here is inferred. Q1 needed no decision.

### ⚠️ Seven of these reverse shipped code

Slices 1b, 2 and 8 built the **previous** defaults and are green in the test
suite. These answers undo that work. Listed here so nobody reads a passing test
as agreement with the current decision.

| D-ID | Q | Was built | Now decided | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D89 | Q3 | Slot card shows real level + lead character (slice 1b) | Mock data, to look like Figma; real fields later | Rework `save_slot_card`; `verify_save_load` expectations change |
| D90 | Q4 | Player data made **per slot** (slice 1b) | Revert to one shared `player_data.json`; per-slot later | **Reintroduces a known correctness bug**: loading slot 2 restores slot 1's inventory and stats |
| D91 | Q5 | Auto-save on touch **removed** (slice 1b) | Auto-save on touch stays, in parallel with the save prompt | Two save paths to keep consistent |
| D99 | Q13 | `CLAUDE.md` test instructions corrected (slice 8) | Leave `CLAUDE.md` alone for now | The "154 GUT tests" claim returns to being wrong and misleading |
| D104 | Q18 | Buy one at a time, `Quantity: 1` (slice 2) | Add a quantity selector | New UI, undesigned in Figma |
| D105 | Q19 | "Not Enough Gold" modal from the shared family (slice 2) | Grey out / disable unaffordable rows; no mandatory modal | Remove the modal path, add a disabled row state |
| D108 | Q22 | Blacksmith = furnace + anvil + workbench | **All four** stations, with tabs `Furnace │ Workbench │ Anvil │ Alchemy` | Slice 4 scope grows; tab strip is undesigned in Figma |

D90 is the one worth a second look: the per-slot fix was made *because* shared
player data makes slot selection incorrect. Reverting is a deliberate choice to
carry that bug.

> **2026-10-22:** D89, D90 and D91 were never built and are **cancelled by D413** — the slice 1b work stands.

## 9. Game Menu structure — 2026-10-12

| ID | Kind | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D116 | TECH | **The Game Menu is one scene.** Every window (7 tab panels, sidebar, party column, bottom bar) is a node in that `.tscn`; the script only shows and hides them. The frame is the shared `base_menu.tscn` (also used by the Shop), instanced with editable children: it owns the background, title, an **empty `Sidebar`** (296 wide, `TopSpacer` 48, `MenuSidebar` gaps) and an **empty `Content` slot**; each screen puts its own buttons and panels into them. `UIMenuShell` was deleted. No `add_nav_item()`, no runtime mounting. **The initial state lives in the scene too** (which panel is visible, which tab is pressed); the script hides nothing on `_ready()` and only reacts to presses. Plan: [[game_menu_single_scene_plan]]. |
| D117 | TECH | **Data rows extend from a template that lives in the scene.** Where a list grows with data (stat rows, equipment slots, party cards, item lists), one real row node sits in the `.tscn` — at least one character always exists, so it is also the first row — and further rows are duplicated from it. | D389 | TECH | **`verify_souls_combat` [0] measures control, not distance** (remaining_work Code 4; agent call). The ~1-in-10 fail was not a lock: each "stuck" second held a designed 0.6 s finisher stagger (D264) plus its 50 px push — the hero was never controllable and still. No stagger chains either (i-frames 0.5 s; none observed in 72 instrumented seconds). [0] now counts a second as stuck only if the hero was free (not staggered) ≥ 0.25 s of it and moved < 150 px/s while free; measured minimum free speed 337 px/s. Gamma = 2^((b − 50) / 50): 0 → 0.5 (darker), 100 → 2.0 (brighter). Black stays black and white stays white; there is no per-scene code. At 50 the layer is hidden, so it costs nothing. | D440 | OVERRIDE | **The Journal lists only people worth knowing, not passers-by** (Sapega, playtest 2026-10-23: «ми не зустрічали віледжера, він тут не потрібен, і це не якийсь важливий НПЦ, про якого буде цікаво щось дізнатись»). `VILLAGER_1` moved from `journal_people.json` → `people` to `not_listed`. `JournalRecords.people()` skips `not_listed` ids, so a save that already recorded one stops showing it. Rule for new speakers: a nameless role (villager, bandit, guard of a crowd) goes to `not_listed`; a character with a name and a story gets an entry. `verify_journal_sections` [6]. |
| D441 | OVERRIDE | **Skills by profession, as in Dragon Nest** (Sapega, playtest 2026-10-23: «панель скілів з професіями не має залежати від панелі скілів без неї, і треба зробити розбивку по професіях як в Драгон Нест»). Each profession gets its own page (a tab) with its own tree: the base class (Holy Warrior), then each oath (Paladin, Dark Knight, Warden of Balance), and later the subclass and prestige (Q66). One page never depends on another's layout. Today the oath trees share the base tree's level grid in one panel (`skills_component`; `design/shots/rows_2_menu_skills_before_after.png`). The look is drawn in Figma first (Claude draws, D436 precedent); the JP / unlock rules do not change. |

The NPC stands at his station from the start, and the hero starts the talk that gives the task. The two first tasks (D293) are side quests, so they get blue. The marker is UI art (pixel "!" in the two colours, not ComfyUI). |

This supersedes D197's pass-through. Walking or jumping into an enemy stops the hero at the enemy's body. The roll (D200, Shift / RB) passes through, as it does through attacks. An enemy still never stands on the hero's head, and the hero never stands on an enemy's: whoever lands on the other slides off. Damage stays attack-only (no contact damage). |

| D456 | OVERRIDE | **An attack can be cut short by a roll or a block** (Sapega, playtest 2026-10-23: «я не можу одночасно бити і зробити перекат, це не зручно, або бити і швидко поставити блок»). During any player attack (the combo hits, the heavy, a skill's wind-up excepted), pressing roll (Shift / RB) or block cancels the rest of the swing at once and starts the roll or the block. If the hit's active frames have not landed yet, it deals no damage. A roll or block pressed a few frames early is buffered. This loosens D200 / D264 timing, but the i-frames and the block rules stay. Add a verify check: an attack cancelled by a roll leaves the hero rolling within 2 physics frames. |

One shared mechanism every cutscene can call: `Cutscene.react(actor, kind)`. There is an emote above the head (pixel UI icons: "!" surprise, "?" confusion, "…" silence, a sweat drop for fear). Surprise adds a short hop back; pain adds a recoil, a red flash (as enemies' hurt flash) and a small shake. No new character frames are needed. | D495 | GAP | **Enemy variety: sizes, elites and shield-bearers** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «моби мають бути різної висоти, і елітні моби якісь мають бути, і з щитом»). Small, medium and large variants of each kind, with scaled shapes and stats. Rare elites have a gold name plate, an aura, one signature move and a better drop. **Break the guard first** (Sapega, follow-up: «за спину ми нікому не зможемо забігти, але спочатку розбити щит, а потім вже наносити нормальні удари; і так зі скорпіоном — треба йому збити броню, а не просто жирний моб»). One shared mechanic: a guard bar above the health bar. The shield-bearer's shield and the scorpion's (tank, D229) armour plates absorb all hits until the bar is gone. Heavy attacks drain it faster. When it breaks, pieces fly off, the enemy staggers, and it then takes normal damage. There is no backstab route (bodies block, D443). The scorpion's bulk moves from HP into armour. Plan: [[enemy_variety_plan]]; numbers are Q85. |

Today there are 20 flat passives, each one stat + value per rank (`skills.json` `passive`), and no synergies. **Synergies:** each active skill takes a bonus from 1–2 passives of its own tree ("Receives bonuses from: Mending Hands +8 % per rank"); a new `synergies: [{skill, per_rank}]` key. **Tooltip:** the current rank's values, the next rank's values, and the synergy lines. **Conditional passives:** some flat passives become conditional, for example "after a roll the next hit +30 %" or "healing +40 % below 30 % HP", using the D207 effect hooks. **More passives than actives** (Sapega: «пасивки теж мають бути, і їх більше, ніж активок»). Every tree has 5 passives and 3 actives today. The synergy pass adds 2–3 conditional passives per tree, so each tree ends at about 7–8 passives to 3 actives (the profession ultimate of D491 included). All values are provisional, tuned in playtest. Agent call on which skill feeds which; Sapega to confirm. |

| D519 | GAP (`cloud/combat-feel`) | **What you see is what hits, as built (B128; agent calls, Sapega to confirm).** Every enemy kind has two shapes of its own (`resource_local_to_scene`, nothing shared), measured from its frames by `Scripts/Combat/HitAreas.gd` and mirrored with its facing (`DefaultEnemy._face_shapes`). **Hurtbox** (new `Hurtbox` Area2D, group `hurtbox`) = the opaque bounds of the `idle` + `walk` frames, weapon and cape included: bandit 70.5×75, jackal 67.5×42, scorpion 74×52, archer 66×75, Brass Collector 120×153, Fire Djinn 51×163.5. The hero's swings and arrows hit it (`DamageApplier` / `Arrow` take `area_entered` on a hurtbox as a hit on its enemy; `hit_targets` still allows one hit per swing), and skills hit it too (`SkillTargeting.enemies_in_front`). The **blocking body** stays the old torso (32×32; bosses 56×100 and 40×100). If the body were the whole drawn bounds, the bandit's own scimitar (34.5 px) could never reach past its 36-px half-width. **Attack hitbox** = the frames shown while it is live (`attack` from `attack_strike_time` for `attack_active_time`), from the enemy's centre forward: bandit 34.5 px, jackal 34.5 (+ its 60-px lunge), scorpion 40.5, Brass Collector 63 (union with `charge_strike`), Djinn 60. **`attack_range` only starts the strike** and is set where the strike lands on a hero standing still: melee 100 → **40**, tank 100 → **46**, fast stays 80. Boss `melee_reach` = the hitbox front (130 → 63, 120 → 60), combo `max_range` 130 → **69**, 120 → **66**. **Hero:** the hitbox is the part of the drawn slash trail in front of the hero's centre, as tall as the trail (`WeaponView.drawn_swing_rect` of the regular first swing; `PlayerCombat.hitbox_center`; the part of an arc that curls behind the hero does not hit, so a swing facing away misses; the finisher's bigger arc stays emphasis). From the hero's centre: bare hands 13–67 px × 10 tall, sword 0–78 × 140, mace 0–66.5 × 117, halberd 34–134 × 17, dagger 32–77 × 8. Thrusts and the punch streak are thin, so they hit a narrow band at chest height. If that feels too strict in play, it is a Sapega call (widen the drawn streaks, not the hitbox). `items.json` `weapon_profile` loses `reach` / `height` (cooldown and knockback stay). **Debug:** F9 in a debug build draws bodies (white), hurtboxes (yellow) and hitboxes (red, filled while live): `Scripts/Combat/HitAreaDebug.gd`, node `Game.tscn → HitAreaDebug`. Tests: `verify_hit_areas` [1] scenes, [2] each weapon against the actually drawn trail, hit at 3 px inside and miss at 6 px past, [3] the same for the bandit, jackal and scorpion strikes. |
| D520 | GAP (`cloud/combat-feel`) | **Bodies block, as built (D443, B125; agent calls).** The D197 exception (`add_collision_exception_with(player)` in `DefaultEnemy._ready`) is gone: walking or jumping into an enemy stops the hero at its torso. **The roll** adds an exception with every enemy at its start (`PlayerMover._pass_through_enemies`). After the roll each exception is removed only once the two bodies no longer overlap (`_release_passed`), so physics never has to push them apart, which could throw one on top. **Nobody stands on anyone's head:** `CombatBody2D.slide_off_bodies(delta)` runs after every `move_and_slide()` of the hero (`PlayerMover`) and of every enemy (`DefaultEnemy`, so the bosses too). A body standing on another `CombatBody2D` (a 2-px probe below the feet, normal pointing up) moves sideways away from that body's centre at **240 px/s** until it falls off. If the sideways step is blocked (a wall, or a third body such as the hero next to a stack of enemies), it pushes the body below the other way. **Fighters are not platforms** (`platform_floor_layers = 0` in `Player.tscn` and `default_enemy.tscn`; the game has no moving platforms). Whoever stands on another fighter does not ride it, and does not inherit its velocity when it moves away. Found by `verify_kusakam_skills`: a teleported enemy launched the hero at −23 500 px/s. **B125:** the Brass Collector dropped on Kusakam slides to the floor. Its **charge** (the boss's only lunge) runs 210 px and stops at the hero's body, never through it or onto it, and it stays on the floor (rose 0.1 px). `BossLogic` has **no jump** move (combo, charge, slam, fireball, pillar), so no jump needed checking. Enemy bodies still collide with each other as before. Tests: `verify_combat_feel` [1]–[3]; `verify_combat_flow` [0b] (an enemy dropped on the hero slides off, over its neighbours, to the floor). `verify_souls_combat` [0] (the 12-s melee) no longer counts walking into an enemy's body as "stuck". |
| D521 | GAP (`cloud/combat-feel`) | **Attack cancel, as built (D456; agent calls).** Cancellable: a combo swing, the heavy (wind-up and strike), a bow shot (`PlayerCombat.can_cancel_attack`). Not cancellable: a skill's wind-up and release, the stagger, the bow draw (the roll already drops the draw, D382). Roll or block pressed during a cancellable attack ends it at once (`cancel_attack`). The hitbox and damage go off, so a hit that has not landed deals nothing, and an unloosed arrow never flies. The combo starts over, and the finisher's recovery (`attack_cooldown`) is kept. The roll starts in the same physics frame (test: within 2); the block starts on the next one. **Buffer:** a roll pressed while it cannot start (stagger, skill wind-up, no dash charge, the second dash too early) waits **0.1 s = 6 physics frames** (`Player.ROLL_BUFFER`) and starts as soon as it can. The block is held, so it is buffered by holding it. I-frames, parry window and guard rules are unchanged. Tests: `verify_combat_feel` [4]. |

| D536 | GAP (`cloud/combat-player`) | **Attacks commit, as built (D504; amends D223, D456, D521).** A combo swing (`PlayerCombat._swing_phase`) has three phases: wind-up, hit (the hitbox is on only from 0.05/0.3 to 0.13/0.3 of the swing, at least 0.05 s) and recovery. From the ground it plants the hero (`locks_movement`) and pushes him forward **10 px** (the finisher **24 px**, the heavy's strike **18 px**) over 0.08 s. In the air he keeps flying. **Facing** stays locked until the hit is out (`locks_facing` → `PlayerMover.facing_locked`), so turning mid-swing no longer turns the body away from the hitbox (B136). Between swings, a held direction turns the next swing (`PlayerMover.face_input`). **Cancel:** a roll, block or jump cuts a combo swing only once its hit is out. The heavy's wind-up and strike cannot be cut. The heavy now has a planted **recovery state of 0.6 s** (was a free 1.0 s cooldown, critique R1), and a roll or block cuts only its last **0.3 s** (`HEAVY_CANCEL_LAST`). What is left of it becomes `attack_cooldown` (B-9). A bow shot can be cut once its arrow is out. Skill casts and drinking cannot be cut. **Buffers:** roll, light and heavy pressed while busy wait **0.2 s** (`INPUT_BUFFER`), even through a stagger. Jump waits **0.1 s** (`PlayerMover.JUMP_BUFFER`). If the jump is released while it waits, it becomes a short hop. Tests: `verify_combat_player` [1], [2]. The D456 checks in `verify_combat_feel` [4] are retired. |
| D537 | OVERRIDE (`cloud/combat-player`) | **Stamina, as built (D504; reverses D264's "no stamina").** `Player/Components/PlayerStamina.gd`, a node in `Player.tscn`. Max 100. Costs: light swing 12, heavy 25 (paid at the wind-up), quick shot 8, charged shot 25, roll 20, blocked hit 15. Regen: 60/s once **0.5 s** pass without the hero acting (any combat state but READY or BLOCK, or a roll). While blocking it regenerates at 30 %. **Empty:** no roll, and a **weak block**: a blocked normal hit deals 50 % damage and staggers (`Player.WEAK_BLOCK_DAMAGE`). Hits that already break the guard are unchanged. Attacks still work on an empty bar. The parry costs nothing. Death and respawn refill it. HUD: an **ST** row under SP in the hero panel (`UITokens.STAMINA_FILL` #e67e22), fed by `EventBus.player_stamina_changed`. All numbers are provisional (table below). Tests: `verify_combat_player` [3]. |
| D538 | OVERRIDE (`cloud/combat-player`) | **Potion quick slot, as built (D504; amends D286).** A new input action `quick_potion`: **Q** / **D-pad right**, listed in Misc → Controls and Settings → Controls. One press drinks a healing potion in real time (`Player.drink_potion` → `PlayerCombat.drink`). The hero must be on the ground and free. He is planted for **0.8 s** and heals at **0.5 s**. The potion leaves the bag at once, so a hit before the heal wastes it. If HP is full, or he is in the air or busy, nothing happens. **Which potion** (agent call): the smallest one that covers the missing HP, otherwise the strongest one carried (`Combat/QuickPotion.gd`). The hero panel shows `Q  Potions ×N` (every healing potion together). The Game Menu no longer drinks healing potions: Enter, double-click or pad A shows "Drink it in play with the quick slot (Q)" in the bottom bar. **The Ether (SP) is still drunk in the menu** (agent call: it is not a heal; Q-ID below). Sound: `potion` (reuses `cast_heal_release.ogg`). Tests: `verify_combat_player` [4], `verify_use_item` [1]–[2]. |
| D539 | GAP (`cloud/combat-player`) | **No HP bar over ordinary enemies, as built (D502).** `DefaultEnemy` no longer creates a HealthBar (its `health_bar` field and `_initialize_health_bar` are gone), and `Scenes/UI/enemy_health_bar.tscn` is deleted. Bosses keep their top-of-screen bar (D316, `BossEnemy`). No elites exist in the code yet, so the gold name plate (D495) is left for `cloud/combat-enemies`, along with the guard bar. The `ENEMY` mode of `HealthBar.gd` is now unused. Tests: `verify_combat_player` [5], `verify_bosses`. |
| D540 | GAP (`cloud/combat-player`) | **The faster combat tutorial, as built (D503).** Four lessons (`CombatTraining.LESSONS`): **Attack** (a combo whose finisher lands; this merges attack and combo), **Dodge or Block** (a dash through a live blow, a block or a parry), **Parry**, **Heavy**. One successful action each. A lesson shows only its banner: the topic is marked seen, so no paused window opens, but it still goes into the Tutorial list. The next lesson starts at once, with a coin sound and no `NEXT_DELAY`. The **cutscene-skip** button (Tab / pad Y) skips the rest (`CombatTraining._unhandled_input`), and the first banner says so. Texts in `tutorial.json`, `en.po` and `uk.po`, with the six old titles and texts replaced. The pad run, each move once, took **8.3 s**. Tests: `verify_onboarding` [1], [2] (under 60 s), [2b] (skip). |
| D541 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **How D488 is built** (`cloud/progression`). `XPManager.MAX_LEVEL = 20`. `is_max_level()` is new, and at the cap `xp_for_next_level` is 0 and `get_xp_percentage()` is 1. `add_xp` at the cap does nothing (no `xp_gained`). A save from the old cap (level > 20) loads at 20 with 0 XP (`restore`). Each bar shows `tr("MAX")` (uk «МАКС») at the cap: the HUD vitals XP row is full with MAX, `XPBar` shows MAX, and the Status "next level" value shows MAX. **The Mayor Hall gate:** `City.tscn` gets a script (`Scenes/City.gd`) and a `MayorHallGate` StaticBody2D (32×480 at x 2560, the east edge before the MetSys exit to MayorHall). Its collision is on while `SkillManager.get_oath() == ""` and turns off on `EventBus.oath_sworn`. `GuardLeft` and `GuardRight` (`gate_guard.tscn`, x 2440 and 2520, sprites flipped to face west; the node is not scaled to −1, so the bubble text is not mirrored) stand by it. `GateTalk` (an Area2D in front of the door): when the hero enters without an oath, `GuardRight` says `City_MayorHall_Guard.dqd` as a passing bubble (`say_in_passing`, D338): «Only the sworn may stand before the mayor.» / «До мера пускають лише тих, хто склав присягу.» [ЧЕРНЕТКА]. The objective becomes "Swear an oath with the oath keepers" (uk «Складіть присягу перед хранителями присяг») with a new `ObjectiveTargets/oath_keepers` marker at the Oath Priest. The City sets that objective on entry while there is no oath. After the oath it goes back to "Find the laboratory". **Provisional placement:** the guards stand in front of the Dervish's tent, because the City's east end has no free ground. The gated porch (ComfyUI, City style) will fix that, and the guards move with it. |
| D542 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **How D447's meditation is built** (`cloud/progression`). `SavePoint._on_interacted` moves the hero onto the point **horizontally only** (it used to move him to the circle's height, so he dropped from mid-air; now he sits on the floor where he stands, and the saved position is on the floor). It then disables movement, plays `play_body(&"Meditate")`, calls `Game.meditate()` (D492), waits `MEDITATE_SIT_TIME` = 0.8 s (a timer that also runs while the tree is paused), and opens the save screen. When the SaveGameState leaves the tree, he plays Idle and gets his movement back. A second interact while he sits does nothing (`meditating`). The `meditation_started` and `meditation_finished` signals are there for the future spot art and sound. Meditating counts even if the player backs out without saving. The blue "S" circle stays until the meditation-spot art is made. |
| D543 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **How D492's respawn rule is built** (`cloud/progression`; amends D152 and D306 "a won fight never comes back"). The regions are data in `Resources/Data/regions.json` (prologue: Canyon, Village, ElderHouse; desert_road: DesertRoad; city: CityGates, City, MayorHall; laboratory: LabCells, Laboratory, LabBoss, LaboratoryOutside), read by `Scripts/Systems/Regions.gd`. `RoomEnemySpawner.respawns` (default false) marks an ordinary fight. It is true for `Fight_Dunes`, `Fight_Mesa`, `Fight_Caravan`, `Fight_Upper`, `Fight_Middle` and `Fight_Lower`. Story fights (`EnemySpawner` in Village, DesertRoad and CityGates) and bosses (not spawners) are untouched. When a respawning fight starts, it registers in `room_state[room]["ordinary_fights"]` as {key: region}, so the list is saved with the slot. `Game.reset_ordinary_fights(region)` erases those keys: `Game.meditate()` erases all of them, and `Game.load_room` erases the target region's keys when the hero crosses into a different region. A spawner already live in the open room stops recording when its fight is reset, so a fight left mid-way after meditating starts fresh next time. Moving between rooms of the same region keeps the state (D152 within a region). **A won fight stays won for the story:** when the reset erases a fight that was cleared, it leaves `<key>_won` in `room_state`, and `RoomEnemySpawner.fight_won()` is `fight_cleared()` or that mark. `Laboratory.is_fight_cleared` now uses `fight_won()`. Without this, meditating at `SavePoint_PreBoss` brought the upper and middle corridors back and `lab_corridors_complete` could never be reached. **A consequence to know:** `SavePoint_PreBoss` stands west of `Fight_Lower`'s zone, so after a save (and so after every boss retry from Game Over) the hero fights through the lower corridor again before the djinn. That is the Tails of Iron and Hollow Knight pattern D492 asks for. If it is too much before a boss, the save point can move east of that zone (D173 placement call). |
| D544 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **The XP curve for the cap** (D492 pacing; `cloud/progression`). `xp_curve.json` changes from `first_level`/`step` to a table `to_next` (XP from level N to N+1, N = 1…19): **100, 150, 200, 250** (D297, unchanged: level 2 on the road, level 5 on the last City Gates kill) → **105, 110, 110, 115, 115, 120, 120, 125, 125, 130, 130** (levels 5→16) → **190, 210, 230, 250** (16→20). Today's straight route gives 2040 XP: Desert Road 625, City Gates 710, Brass Collector +400 (1110, level 8), corridors +430 (1540, level 12), djinn +500 (2040, **level 16**). Two rounds of the respawning corridors (430 each) reach **20**, and one round is not enough. The cost per level drops after level 5 (250 → 105). This is deliberate: D297 fixes the first 700 XP, and after the City there are only 4 XP sources. When D492's standing groups land, the straight route gains XP. `verify_xp_curve` [4] then fails if the Laboratory's end goes past 17, and the 5→16 band should be raised so the curve rises steadily again. |

| D547 | GAP (`cloud/world-fixes`) | **How D483 is built.** Each outdoor room (Village, Canyon, DesertRoad, CityGates, City, LaboratoryOutside) has `metadata/camera_floor_y = 384` on its root. After `adjust_camera_limits`, `Game._raise_camera_bottom()` sets `limit_bottom = floor + 15 % of the view height` (456 at 480 units) and lowers `limit_top` so that one full view fits (−24). The floor then sits at 85 % of the screen. In the 2-cell-high rooms (Village, Canyon, Laboratory Outside) the camera used to follow the hero down and showed about 45 % ground. Now it shows 15 %. Both desert backdrops have a new `Plan5/SkyTop` strip (the top 4 px row of the sky, stretched to 96 units above y 0), so the view above the room shows sky with no blank. The Canyon exit cut-scene sets the bottom limit back to the room's full height before the climb down, so the hero stays in view below the floor. Indoor rooms have no metadata and keep the MetSys cell limits: they have no backdrop above their top edge, so raising their camera would show blank. That needs art or a separate call. |

| D572 | GAP | **The September questions are closed with the defaults as built** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «так, закрий»). | D573 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **What each tell colour means, as built** (D504; `cloud/combat-enemies`). `EnemyLogic.TELL_LEAD = 0.45` s before the hit, the weapon glints and `EventBus.enemy_tell_shown` plays a sound (`tell_block`: high metal tick; `tell_parry`: high bell; `tell_dodge`: low plate thud; `sound_events.json`). The parry window is still the last `PARRY_WINDOW` (0.25 s) before the hit, so the glint warns and is no longer the deadline. **White (block):** a block holds it; a block in the parry window is still only a block. **Yellow (parry):** a block in the window parries it; a later block holds it, unless the hit would guard-break (stagger ≥ 0.6 s, as before), so a heavy yellow hit is parried or eaten. **Red (dodge):** neither block nor parry holds it; it hits in full and staggers. The colour is decided in `PlayerCombat.guard_against` (`DefaultEnemy.incoming_tell()`, `Arrow.tell`). The direction locks at the tell (it used to lock at 0.25 s). This amends D200 ("no parry colours") and D264. |
| D574 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Tell per enemy and per move** (D504; `cloud/combat-enemies`). `EnemyStats.tell` / `finisher_tell`: bandit yellow, then a red finisher; jackal white, then a yellow finisher; scorpion red; archer yellow (arrows can be parried, D384). Bosses: each move has `tell` (charge, slam, fireball, pillar: red) and the combo has `tells` per swing. Brass Collector: yellow, red (phase 2: yellow, yellow, red). Djinn: yellow, yellow (phase 2: yellow, white, red). The tutorial (lesson_dodge, lesson_parry), Controls and the archer's bestiary entry now say "red glint = dodge" and "yellow glint = parry as the blow lands", in `en.po` and `uk.po`. |
| D575 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Parry limits and the riposte** (D504, B133; `cloud/combat-enemies`). Boss hazards pass themselves as the damage source (`BossFireball`, `BossPillar`, and the slam's shockwave node), so `guard_against` returns nothing and the boss is never staggered by a parry from afar. Hazards no longer stagger the hero either: `Player.take_damage` staggers only from enemies and arrows, and `Player.gd` was not touched. Riposte: parrying a `DefaultEnemy` (not an arrow, and not with a bow) sets `PlayerCombat.riposte_left = RIPOSTE_WINDOW` (1.0 s). The next attack or heavy press from READY is `_riposte`: the HeavyStrike frames at once with no wind-up, `RIPOSTE_DAMAGE` ×3, `RIPOSTE_POISE` 999 ("ignores poise": it breaks any poise, the boss's included), heavy knockback, then `heavy_recovery`. A stagger, a cancel or leaving the tree closes the window. |
| D576 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Committed swings, recovery, token, spacing** (D504, B134, critique §B; `cloud/combat-enemies`). After the tell, `EnemyLogic.committed` is true: knockback and stuns no longer break the swing; only `stagger` (poise break, parry) does. Before the tell, knockback still breaks it (D208). Bosses have `knockback_immune`: no push from hits, the poise-break stagger included. After every series the enemy holds its strike frame for `EnemyStats.recovery` s (`EnemyView.hold_recovery`), then backs off `back_off` px, facing the hero (bandit 80, jackal 120, scorpion 0). The back-off times out after `back_off / speed + 0.4` s when an ally blocks the way. Attack token: `MAX_ATTACKERS = 2` enemies hold it from the first wind-up until the recovery starts; bosses neither take nor count it. Between attacks a melee enemy holds `attack_range + WAIT_GAP` (40 px), shuffling ±12 px at half speed. It steps in only when it is ready and a token is free, or at once when the hero starts a heavy or a cast within 1.5× reach. The archer engages from its own `attack_range`. |
| D577 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Boss move patterns** (D504; `cloud/combat-enemies`). `BossEnemy.pattern` / `pattern_phase2` are queues of move kinds. `BossLogic.pick_move` takes the next kind in the queue that fits the distance and phase and is not the last move. A repeat is allowed only when nothing else fits; the queue restarts on a phase change. When nothing fits, the boss walks in. Between moves it walks to `preferred_range` (±30 px) at 0.6× speed. Brass: `combo, slam, combo, charge`; phase 2: `combo, charge, slam, combo, slam`; preferred 60 px. Djinn: `fireball, pillar, combo, fireball, pillar`; phase 2: `pillar, fireball, combo, pillar, fireball`; preferred 300 px. `weight` is gone. Combo length is fixed by `tells`. Phase 2 adds a swing to each combo and a second fireball and side pillars, as before. Provisional, for Sapega's play. |
| D578 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Noticing and ledges** (B139; `cloud/combat-enemies`). `EnemyLogic.can_notice`: feet within ±64 px (`NOTICE_DY`), or a clear ray from enemy to hero (enemies and hero excluded). It gates the detection circle, the idle check and the chase. An unseen hero during a chase counts as lost (3 s, then home), and the enemy stands still meanwhile. `can_step(dir)`: no wall within 8 px of the body's edge, and floor no deeper than 16 px under the feet. It is used by every walk (chase, step in, spacing, back-off, home) and replaces the archer's own check. An enemy that cannot get home (knocked over a ledge) makes its current spot its new home. |
| D579 | OVERRIDE | **The player fantasy: a cowardly but brave little lizard villager who becomes a paladin** (Sapega, 2026-10-23, answering "з чого будується цікавий геймдизайн → ясна фантазія гравця"; full quote in [[lore]]). Kusakam comes from a family of shamans, and his grandfather made him study shamanism; instead he slowly discovers a paladin's powers. He is a weak, naive villager from the edge of the worlds, where only desert folk lived, guarded by a barrier. The barrier fell asleep, and the bandits found them. He is very cowardly, but brave. **What it steers:** growth from clumsy to paladin across the 20 levels and the skill trees (D535); his manner, which is scared but stepping forward (idle, hurt and low-HP poses, his lines); the grandfather's shaman teaching against the paladin path (Healing Palm from the grandfather, D140); his wonder at the first City and its traders; and the sleeping barrier as a story thread. |
| D580 | OVERRIDE | **Combat is the first pillar: invest in it as Tails of Iron did** (Sapega, 2026-10-23, on the core loop: «Якщо бій нудний, жодна карта не врятує, тому Tails of Iron вклав усе в бій — ми теж маємо в це вкластись»). The moment-to-moment loop of strike, dodge and parry comes before maps and menus when work is ordered. The next waves put combat first: removing stamina (D551), enemy variety (D560), the new Laboratory boss fight (D564), hero animations (one sword hand B124, the bow in every pose B110, the death D138) and a hit-feel pass. Each combat change is judged in a real fight capture, not only in a green test. |
| D581 | OVERRIDE | **Fair difficulty and readable feedback: we need more of it** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «нам цього поки не вистачає, і це нам треба»). A death must read as "I saw the wind-up and didn't roll". On top of the colour tells (D573, D574), every hit carries weight:<br>- hitstop and knockback scaled to the blow;<br>- screen shake on heavy blows and on hits the hero takes;<br>- a sound for each tell and each kind of hit;<br>- the hero's hurt flash.<br>A wind-up is always visible before damage. Provisional idea for Sapega: the death screen names the blow that killed (the enemy and its tell colour). Part of the combat pillar (D580). |
| D582 | OVERRIDE | **New enemies come in portions; crowds are hard** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «треба поступово додавати ворогів, не одразу усіх, але якщо їх багато, то це має бути складно»). Each new kind (shield-bearer, archer, vulture, bat, elite) first appears **alone**, somewhere safe enough to learn it, and the room teaches it without text. After that it comes **in pairs and mixes** (shield-bearer in front, archer behind). **Big groups are rare and hard:** optional fights guarding a reward, or the stretch before a boss. Combat pacing runs fight → quiet → secret → boss. |
| D583 | OVERRIDE | **Curiosity is rewarded; no free gifts on the road** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «продумай це і для нашої гри… просто подарунки на дорозі — це якось мерзко»). Nothing lies free on the main path. Items come from **secrets** (a branch, a hidden room, a breakable wall, a climb), **chests** guarded or hidden, **optional fights**, **NPC quests** and enemy drops. Every "what's there?" ends in a **chest**, a **piece of story** (a note, a mural, the grandfather's or the alchemist's papers) or a **shortcut** back to a meditation spot. The road pickups that exist today (`Pickup_DuneLedge`, `Pickup_MesaLedge`, `Chest_CaravanLedge`) move into branches. Per-location layout: [[world_structure_plan]] → *Curiosity map*. |
| D584 | OVERRIDE | **Growth eases the game a little but never makes it easy** (Sapega, 2026-10-23, against "the player must see he got stronger": «мені стає трошки легше, але в цілому має бути складно завжди: не з першого удару вмираю, але ухилитись від усього все одно не можу і вмираю»). Levels, gear and skills buy survivability and options: more hits before death, more ways to answer. They never remove the need to read and dodge. Enemies in later locations hit as hard relative to the hero's growth (D494 numbers per location), the level cap of 20 (D541) and the XP curve keep over-levelling small, and an old boss stays dangerous when fought again. Tuned in playtest. |
| D585 | OVERRIDE | **The four pillars** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «о так, це наші стовпи»): 1) hard, readable combat; 2) locations with secrets; 3) a build through profession and skills; 4) companions. With the player fantasy (D579) they live in [[pillars]], the note to read before any game-design call. A feature that serves no pillar is cut, and conflicts are settled in pillar order. |
| D586 | OVERRIDE | **The story spine: the stolen Maiden and the sleeping barrier** (Sapega, 2026-10-23; quote in [[lore]]). The bandits stole **the Maiden of Heavenly Punishment** («діва кари небесної»). The theft starts the long road and does not end the story. The barrier slept because nobody prayed to the relic, so the Maiden had no strength. The world has **no angels and no demons**, only spirits, gods and demigods, followers of the divine path. A dragon is equal to a god, perhaps stronger, and nobody has checked (Sapega, the same day). Nobody sent the bandits: they saw the villagers guarding something, stole it and took the villagers as slaves (Sapega). The relic is a statuette and the Maiden lives in it. Kusakam's paladin powers are her influence; she does not care which oath he swears (D570) and wants him to find a worthier place for her and her followers, which is the long road (Sapega). Every text, journal entry and enemy description keeps to this. Story beats about the Maiden are drafted with `[ЧЕРНЕТКА]` until Sapega writes them; the open points are listed in [[lore]]. |
| D587 | OVERRIDE | **The main plot: the blue sky is a curse** (Sapega, 2026-10-23: «основний сюжет в тому, що синє небо — це прокляття невідомої сили: хто на нього подивиться, в того синіють очі, і потім він втрачає розсудок і стає як зомбі»). An unknown power cursed the sky. Whoever looks at it gets blue eyes, loses their mind and becomes like a zombie. This is the main thread above the Maiden's road (D586). The caster is very powerful and is never revealed: the curse only keeps getting in the way, an obstacle on the road and never a villain to unmask or fight (Sapega, the same day). The Maiden's barrier shields her followers from the curse (Sapega: «так»), so a worthier place for her means a place where the barrier can shield them again. Alisia's blue eye is chance, and that is why the alchemist injected her: he experiments in search of a cure for the curse (Sapega). The open point (the blue-eyed mindless as enemies) are listed in [[lore]]. The finale's camera pan into the sky (D473) now reads as ominous, not triumphant: its tone is checked with Sapega before it is built. |

| D627 | OVERRIDE | **JP caps for 19 skills a tab: base 14, profession 12** (D530 re-tuned; agent call, provisional). Learning a whole tab costs 23 JP in the base tree and 26 in each profession. The hero has 19 JP at level 20. Both caps sit well under a full tab (61 % and 46 %), so even a higher level cap would not max a tab. Their sum (26) is 7 over the 19 JP the hero has, so where those JP go is the build choice. One way is 10 base JP (the ultimate's gate) + 9 profession JP, which reaches the ultimate path (Radiant Smite 1 + Skyfall 2 + Judgement 2 = 5) and 4 more. Another is 14 base + 5 profession, which gives the whole trunk and the ultimate path. A third is 7 base + 12 profession, the widest profession but no ultimate. Today's built skills cost 14 in the base tree (equal to its cap) and 16 in each profession (over the cap). |

| D668 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **The difficulty simulation** (D642; `cloud/difficulty`). `verify_difficulty.gd` walks the story route (Canyon → Village → Desert Road → City Gates → City → Mayor Hall → Lab cells → Laboratory → LabBoss). For each fight it takes the hero's level at the fight's start (XP from the spawn points' EnemyStats, as `verify_xp_curve`) and builds a real hero at that level with the route's gear: before the City the grandfather's copper sword and leather armour, from the City the shops' iron sword, iron armour and iron shield. It then spawns the real enemy scene (medium size, elite where the fight has `elite = always` or `elite_rolls`) or the boss scene. **H** is how many of the enemy's ordinary hits (for a boss, a combo swing) kill the hero through `Player.mitigated_damage`, with no block and no dodge (the evasion roll is calmed). **N** is how many hero actions kill it: the light combo via `PlayerCombat.hit_damage` with no crit, from behind (tank shell, shield), plus the best affordable active (`SkillManager.skill_damage` + burn) whenever SP allows. The fight starts at **0 SP**, and SP comes from hits (D504). Two builds: **STRENGTH** puts every attribute point in Strength and spends no JP. **SKILLS** puts 2 Strength / 2 Vitality / 1 Wisdom per level and learns in order Toughness, Shield Block, Weapon Training, Radiant Smite, Devotion, Fire Strike, Riposte Instinct, Aura of Resolve, Mending Hands, Sky's Grace, Skyfall, Fortitude, Last Stand (Paladin oath from level 5), with leftover JP into active ranks. **Wins:** a fight of N hero hits costs ceil(N / OPENINGS) blows taken (OPENINGS = hits a good player lands per blow he eats: boss 10, elite 4), and the hero must survive them with the full flask (Healing Palm, all charges). Asserts: [0] each fight's `enemy_level` = hero level there; [1] ordinary, SKILLS: H 5–6, N 4–6; [2] boss, SKILLS: H 3–4, N 34–46; [3] from the oath (level 5) on, SKILLS wins every elite and boss fight and STRENGTH loses. Before the oath both builds hold ≤ 4 JP and no active skill, so the road's lone elite (level 2) must be won by either. [4] A boss's heaviest move never kills a full-HP hero in one hit. [5] each 10 Strength adds less Phys. Atk. |
| D669 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **A level is worth less; Strength has diminishing returns** (D642). `xp_curve.json` gets `hp_per_level` **10** (was 20) and `damage_per_level` **2** (was 5), which used to be hardcoded in `XPManager.stat_bonuses`. `attributes.json` Strength: `"diminishing": {"step": 10, "factor": 0.5}`: points 1–10 give +1 Phys. Atk. each, 11–20 +0.5, 21–30 +0.25, and so on (`AttributeManager.effective_points`). The skills screen's "+1 gives" preview shows the next point's real gain. At level 12 a Strength-only hero gets +19 from 55 points (was +55). |

| D690 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Skill ranks in the data** (D674; `cloud/skills-dn`). Each skill in `skills.json` has `rank: {max, jp, bonus}`. **max** is 5 for actives (ultimates and Healing Palm too) and 3 for passives. **jp** is the price of each rank above the first: the skill's learning cost, at least 1 (1 JP for skills up to level 10, 2 JP from level 12). **bonus** is the share of the rank-1 effect that each rank above the first adds: **+15 %** for actives (damage, heal and burn, so ×1.6 at rank 5) and **+25 %** for passives (so ×1.5 at rank 3). This replaces D150 (rank 20, +10 %, 1 JP) and D626's "+10 % per rank" for passives. `SkillManager.MAX_RANK / RANK_BONUS / UPGRADE_COST` are gone; `check_upgrade`, `tree_spent`, `spent_points`, `SkillPlan` and `SkillTooltip` read the data. Old saves: `SkillManager.fit_ranks()` (called by `PlayerDataModule` on load) trims a rank above the cap and refunds 1 JP per trimmed rank, the price under D150. The JP caps (14 / 12, D627) now bound ranks too, so maxing is a choice: the whole base tree's ranks would cost far more than 14. D642 still holds: the skill build of `verify_difficulty` ranks its actives with leftover JP, and its results are unchanged (the 2 Brass Collector fails predate this branch, B144). Numbers provisional. |

So `RoomDoor` gets `gate_id` (a **flag** gate from gates.json: while the flag is missing, E plays its `locked_dialogue` instead of entering), `sets_flag` (going through sets a quest flag: a shortcut opened from inside) and `cover` (a `BreakableWall` in the background that hides the door: no prompt until a heavy blow breaks it; `BreakableWall.was_broken()`). The Canyon's `Wall_Mural` has `collision_layer = 0` and sits behind the hero. New gates: `lizard_shrine` (key), `camp_stockade` (key), `camp_shortcut` (flag `bandit_camp_shortcut`, on the road's `CampShortcutDoor` by `SavePoint_BeforeCity`, opened by the camp's `ShortcutDoor`), `gatehouse_cellar` (flag `gatehouse_cellar_allowed`, on CityGates' `CellarDoor`). `verify_gates` now also counts gated RoomDoors as "a gate stands in a room". |

| D702 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Release builds keep a readable crash log** (B145; `cloud/b145`). A typed call on null is only an error in the editor but a native crash in a release export (B142, B145), and the release template neither flushed `godot.log` per line nor kept a GDScript call stack. `project.godot`: `application/run/flush_stdout_on_print=true` (every print reaches the file before a crash), `debug/settings/gdscript/always_track_call_stacks=true` (every error in a release build prints `GDScript backtrace … [0] func (file.gd:line)`). `Game.load_room()` prints `🚪 Game: room swap <old> → <new>` before the swap and `🚪 Game: room swap done, <new> in` after it. Cost: a small per-call overhead in the GDScript VM and a flush per print; keep both until the playtest builds stop crashing. Exported builds are driven with `Scripts/Tools/export_drive.sh` (an `override.cfg` autoload; templates ignore `--script`). |

| D707 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **A big archer aims down from its drawn bow** (D652, D386). A big archer's bow is drawn above the head of a standing hero (the heavy archer's arrowhead is 16 px above the hero's 40-px body), so a level arrow from the bow would never hit. `ArcherEnemy.max_tilt` (0 = level, as every ordinary archer still shoots): above 0, the arrow is aimed from the arrowhead at the centre of the hero's body, clamped to ±max_tilt px per px of flight. The heavy archer has 0.25; at 170–380 px that is 0.09–0.21. `Arrow.fall` carries it: the arrow moves along (direction, fall), its sprite and tip are rotated to the line, and a parried arrow flies back along the same line (`fall = -fall`), so it hits the archer's tall hurtbox. Block, parry, roll and jump work as against the level arrow (`verify_archer` [10]). The archer still shoots only when the hero is within `same_height` (48 px) of its height. |

| D715 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **The two-level World Map as built** (D458, D487, D565, D714; `cloud/world-map`). `metsys_map_component.tscn/.gd` (node name kept for `game_menu.tscn` and the tests) draws a 1920×1080 frame over the menu's illustrated map. It no longer has a MetSys `MapView`. **Level 1:** one pin per `locations.json` location at its `map_pos` (top-left of the plate). Pin states: here (red plate and dot, «ви тут»), visited (≥ 1 explored cell; gold, «відвідано»), locked (plate at 60 %, grey name and dot, «закрито»). Left / right walks the pins in screen order left to right. Left from the leftmost pin and B return to the tabs, as in every tab. The hero's location is selected first, and a selected visited pin gets a 3 px gold border. A locked pin can be selected (the card shows it) but does not open. The card shows `Досліджено N% · Скрині a/b` and «A — відкрити карту локації», with the hint line hidden for a locked location. **Level 2:** the menu's map under a `#EDDBB8` 93 % veil, and the title = the location in capitals plus «Карта локації · B — до карти світу». The location's cells (150×84 each) are fitted to the area x 340–1480, y 160–990: centred, and scaled down only when they do not fit. Explored cells are filled with a 3 px ink border; seen cells get a 2 px ink border at 45 %, dashed 10/8; hidden cells are not drawn. A room name goes in its first explored cell (top row, then left), only for explored rooms; a room seen but never entered gets «?». Icons are 26 px circles at the object's place in its cell, in explored cells only. The exception is locked doors and exits, which also show in seen cells, because they are visible from the doorway as in the frame. Passage labels are «↕ драбина» and «↓ спуск» at the bottom of the upper cell. The legend is at (1500, 700). B / Esc returns to level 1, and the arrows keep the focus on the map. **The tab always opens on level 1** (spec: the frames do not say; the tab is called World Map). The party column is hidden while the tab is shown, as in the frames and like Status. **Type:** the frames' Inter lines use Cormorant Garamond, the game's only font. All map text keeps its Figma size (types in `FIGMA_FONT_TYPES`, no ×1.25 from D166), because it sits in fixed plates and 150×84 cells. The pin border is 2 px (StyleBoxFlat takes whole pixels; Figma has 1.5). The glyphs (☗ ✦ ▣ ⚿ ! →) are drawn as shapes, since Cormorant has none of them. **Room titles** use the scene name as words (`LizardCave` → «Lizard Cave»), as `location_title` did before D618. uk: Каньйон, Дім діда, Печера ящерів, Розпис дракона, Табір бандитів, Підйом на Месу (from the frame); Зала мера, Підвал брами, Біля лабораторії, Камери лабораторії, Серце лабораторії (drafts). **No secrets count** (Q98) and **no «↓ криниця»** (Q99). |

| D730 | OVERRIDE | **Every enemy and boss gets anime-style hit effects, like the hero's. Poses are not redrawn** (Sapega, 2026-10-26: «анімації у всіх мають бути анімешними, не тільки у мене», then «перемальовувати не треба — анімації удару я мав на увазі, а не анімації персонажів»; `cloud/enemy-fx`, A17). There is one FX system: `Scripts/FX/EnemyStrikeFX.gd`, a child of `VFXHooks`, reuses `SlashTrail`, `LandingDust` and `ParrySpark` from a fixed pool (16 trails, 6 dust, 6 sparks, round-robin, nothing allocated on a strike). It listens to the new `EventBus.enemy_struck(enemy, move, direction)`, which fires on the strike frame from `DefaultEnemy.start_attack`, `BossEnemy.start_attack`, `BossEnemy.strike` (slam) and `ArcherEnemy.start_attack`. `move` comes from `DefaultEnemy.strike_move()`: swing / lunge / bite / dive / charge / slam / shot. The weapon comes from `enemy_type` (`EnemyStrikeFX.WEAPONS`). The smear covers the strike zone, from the body centre to the far edge of the hitbox. **Bandit, shield bandit, tower-shield guard:** arc, steel blue. **Brute:** club arc ×1.15 plus dust ×1.4 where the club lands, dust tan. **Scorpions (tank, armoured):** a sting thrust, venom green. **Jackals (fast, cursed, pack leader):** a jaw snap (two arcs closing and a punch flash), pale violet; the cursed one is purple. **Bat, fly:** jaw snap, purple. **Vultures:** a dive streak along the dive, feather tan. **Lab mercenary:** mace and club arcs, green, and the slam is a heavy arc ×1.3 plus dust ×2.2 both ways. **Scorpicore:** axe arc ×1.3, venom green. A **lunge, charge or bat bite** adds 3 speed lines behind and a dust kick-off. **Archers:** a streak behind the arrow (`arrow.tscn → Streak`) in its tell colour (D504), and small sparks ×0.35 where it lands (`EventBus.arrow_landed`). Smear colours are `UITokens.ENEMY_SMEAR_*`, alpha 0.9. None of them is the tell white, yellow or red. **Landed hit on the hero:** the existing cut, hitstop, shake, vignette and white flash (D204, D581). It now counts as heavy (`HURT_FEEL[1]`) for an elite and for a blow of ≥ 20 % of the hero's max HP (`VFXHooks.HEAVY_HURT_SHARE`, `hurt_tier_for`); bosses stay at tier 2. **Block:** the existing block spark; **parry:** the parry spark. **Low quality** (D554): smears stay, with 1 speed line, no dust and no arrow sparks. Movement, moves, sizes and balance are unchanged. Test: `verify_enemy_fx` [1]–[4]; capture: `capture_enemy_fx.gd` → `design/shots/enemy_fx.png`. |

New Game gets a new id and an empty list. **Limit:** a tip seen in a session that was then quit without any save (no save point, Auto-Save off) is not on disk, so after a restart it can show once more. |

| D755 | ROUTINE CALL · ✅ Sapega 27.10 | **Why play was easier than paper, and the behaviour that fixes it** (D642; measured in `verify_masher`, report `cloud_reports/hard-fights`). (1) **A swing holds its ground:** during its wind-up and strike an enemy is not pushed by the hero's hits, and a push no longer breaks the swing before its tell; only a poise break or a parry stops it. This amends B134's "knockback before the tell breaks the swing" (`DefaultEnemy.holds_ground`). (2) **An enemy answers a hero who keeps hitting:** `EnemyStats.answer_hits` hits from the hero during the pause between series, or during its back-off, cancel the rest of the pause, and it attacks with its normal wind-up and tell (`EnemyLogic.provoked`). (3) **A fight is one group:** when one enemy of a `RoomEnemySpawner` starts chasing, the idle ones join (`_rouse`). They run at 1.8× pace while farther than 2.5 × engage range (the walk animation speeds up with them), and step in to strike at 1.4× pace. (4) **Ground enemies pass through each other** (the hero is still blocked, D443), so a group in a one-lane road no longer queues behind its front body. (5) **A light swing stops in the first enemy:** a combo swing that is not the finisher damages one target (`DamageApplier.max_targets`); the finisher, the heavy and the riposte still hit everyone in reach. (6) **A flask or potion is an opening:** like a heavy or cast wind-up, the quick-slot potion draw (`DRINK`) makes nearby melee enemies attack at once, and an archer shoots at once from its full range (`EnemyLogic._hero_opened`, `_punish_range`). (7) **Elite traits come from `enemy_variety.json` "elite"**: damage ×2.3 (was 1.5), poise ×1.6, and an elite is not pushed by the hero's hits (like a boss, B134). Elite kinds copy their base kind's numbers again (D703, D709, D711). (8) The hero's post-hit invulnerability is 0.1 s (was 0.5) and a hit pushes him 20 px (was 50), so two enemies that strike together both land. |

| D775 | ADDED (Sapega 26.10, `cloud/enemy-ai`) | **Enemies mean to kill** («інтелект ворогів має бути трохи кращий»; «щоб вони хотіли вбити персонажа, а не просто бачили, рухались і били по повітрю»). The existing enemy logic now hunts the hero (`EnemyLogic`, `BossLogic`, `ArcherLogic`). (1) A swing starts only if it will reach the hero at the strike: his position is predicted from his velocity; the enemy creeps in and turns until the tell and strikes with a step of up to 24 px. A wind-up the hero has rolled or walked out of is dropped before the tell, and a series stops when its next swing cannot reach. After a roll or a jump enemies follow at once: they run after a hero who walks away, lunge at him, and go to where they last saw him. (2) Openings are punished out of rhythm, by one enemy at a time: a heavy's recovery, the end of the hero's combo, the flask, a cast, a landing. Bosses punish only the recovery and the landing. (3) The enemy reads the hero (`EnemyStats.answer_swing`): light kinds hop back out of a heavy's reach (70 %), shield kinds stand shield-first through his swing and answer after it, and armoured kinds trade. Blockable kinds do not swing into a front block: they walk round him or wait, and after 1.2 s of block they test it; the village lessons' enemies always swing into it. Archers back off earlier, pass through him when cornered, and lead their shots. (4) After a series every melee kind steps out of his reach. The rest between series is ×0.7–1.3. A boss with the hero in its combo reach for over 0.7 s strikes. Elites feint once every 2–4 series: a wind-up pose without a tell, cut off; a shown tell still always strikes (D504). In a group only one tell is lit at a time. In the drill, swings at the air fell from 10 % to 2 %, and the longest idle in reach from 1.29 s to 0.70 s. Numbers are provisional. Report: `cloud_reports/enemy-ai`. |
| D776 | OVERRIDE (reverses D551, restores D537; Sapega 26.10, `cloud/stamina-poise-2`) | **Stamina is back** (Sapega 2026-10-26: «а ще все-таки енергію гравцю треба додати, бо бити без передиху — це круто, але тоді ворог не буде встигати вдарити або провести атаку»). `PlayerStamina` (D537's, from `c526ad3e`), max 100. Costs: light swing 18, heavy 30 at its wind-up, quick and charged shots 10 / 25, roll 22, raising the block 5, a blocked hit 10. Parry and riposte are free. Refill: 100/s once 0.4 s pass without spending; half pace while the block is held. At zero the hero is exhausted: no swing, heavy, shot or roll until the bar is back to 30 (0.7 s from empty; full in 1.4 s), and enemies treat him as an opening. Each refused press flashes the ST row red and plays a dull sound. A blocked hit on an empty bar is a weak block (½ damage and a stagger, as D537). Armour weight prices the roll (light −20 %, heavy +20 %) and the refill (light +10 %, heavy −15 %); Sand Runner's Ring makes rolls 30 % cheaper. HUD: the ST row under SP at the Figma D549 revision's place (plate 470 × 144, panel 520 × 220). Tuned with `verify_masher`: a masher runs dry within a combo and a half (3–5 swings), and 49 of the 80 blows he takes land while his bar is empty. All numbers are provisional. |

| D784 | ROUTINE CALL (extends D765, D783) | **Found secrets on the map** (`cloud/secrets-icon`). Sapega 2026-10-27: «В нас на карті будуть відмічатись знайдені секрети…?» → yes. D765's and D783's "no level-2 icon" is replaced: a *found* SecretArea is drawn at its node's position on level 2, in the 26 px circle style. The fill is `UITokens.MAP_ICON_SECRET` (violet `#7A4FB3`, kept well away from the blue meditation fill) and the glyph is a white keyhole (a circle over a trapezoid). It is not ✦, which is the meditation star, and not the padlock bow used for locked. An unfound secret draws nothing, so the map never gives one away. | D785 | ROUTINE CALL (extends D714, D715) | **The World Map is a canvas** (`cloud/map-pan`). Sapega 2026-10-27: «А ми зможемо гуляти мишкою по карті світу? Якось переміщати її, як в Octopath Traveler» → yes, on both levels. **Camera:** screen = offset + point × zoom (`map_camera.gd`). Level 1 moves the menu's own illustrated background (`base_menu.tscn → MapBackground`, group `menu_map_background`) and the pins inside the content area x 320–1920, y 0–1016. The pins ride with the map but keep their size, and they are clipped at the sidebar. Level 2 moves `Grid` inside the existing `Rooms` frame (340–1480 × 160–990). Cells, names and icons scale with the map. The title, the location card, the legend and the new room card stay fixed. **Zoom:** the minimum is the fit. On level 1 that is the smallest scale at which the parchment still covers the content area (0.94). On level 2 it is the whole location in the frame (≤ 1, as D715). The maximum is `MAP_ZOOM_MAX` 2.5, where 1 = the Figma frame / the 150×84 cell. One wheel notch is ×1.15 and zooms around the cursor. The triggers and Q / E zoom at ×2 per second around the centre of the view. **Pan:** a left-button drag follows the cursor 1:1. On release the map glides at the mouse's speed (at most 3000 px/s), slowing as e^(−6t), so it travels about v / 6. There is no glide if the mouse rested for more than 0.1 s before release. WASD and the right stick (dead zone 0.2) pan at 900 px/s. On level 2 the arrows, D-pad and left stick pan too. On level 1 they keep choosing pins (D715), and the view slides to a chosen pin that is within 120 px of the edge. **Edges:** content larger than the view is clamped to its edges; smaller content is centred. A glide that reaches an edge stops there, with no bounce. **Opening:** at the approved frames' scale (1), centred on the hero as far as the edges allow. On level 1 the Canyon pin is near the right edge, so the opening view equals the approved frame. Level 2 opens at the natural cell size, centred on the hero icon. **Hover / click:** a pointer over a pin selects it (gold border and card, as with left / right). A press-and-release that travels ≤ `MAP_DRAG_SLOP` 6 px is a click and opens the pin (a locked pin stays shut, as before). Anything longer is a drag. On level 2 the room under the pointer gets a 4 px `MAP_GOLD` border and a room card at (1500, 160) above the legend. The card shows the room title and «Скрині a/b», or «?» and «бачив вхід, ще не заходив» for a seen room. With keys or a pad, the room under the centre of the view is highlighted. On opening, that is the hero's room. **Keys kept:** Q / E (menu_prev_tab / menu_next_tab) were unused on this tab, so they zoom. LB / RB, which are bound to the same actions, zoom as well. The bottom hint adds «Тягни або стік — рухати карту. Коліщатко або Q / E — масштаб.» The background goes back to the frame when the tab is left (Inventory and the other tabs share it). |

| D812 | ROUTINE CALL (`cloud/skills-v2-move`, D-sv2m) | **Movement skills: dash and leap-slam** (skills v2 slice 3; D535, D443, D504, S19; `cloud/skills-v2-move`).<br><br>**How the move works.** The hero moves between wind-up and strike (`PlayerCombat` `CAST_MOVE`). He moves with his body (`PlayerMover.push` and gravity), so enemy bodies stop him and nobody gets behind an enemy. A blow during the move interrupts the cast (D259): SP is paid and nothing is hit. A movement skill starts without an enemy in front, because it is the way to reach one. It starts only from the ground. **A whiff after moving costs its SP**, because a free reposition would be a free dodge.<br><br>**Dash** (`move.kind dash`): a push of `distance` px over `time` s. With `hits: first` it cuts the nearest enemy the moment one is in strike reach (Dash Strike, Blood Rush, Wind Step). With `hits: front` it runs its whole time into the body, then hits everyone in reach (Shield Charge).<br><br>**Leap** (`kind leap`): up `height` px and forward `distance` px. It lands 24 px before the first enemy ahead (`SkillCombatExecutor.leap_distance`), never behind it. The slam hits everyone within `reach` px on both sides (Heaven's Anvil, Dune Quake).<br><br>**Provisional numbers:**<br>• Dash Strike: 180 px / 0.18 s, cuts the first enemy.<br>• Shield Charge: 220 px / 0.35 s, knockback 120 px.<br>• Blood Rush: 200 px / 0.2 s, heals 30 % of its damage.<br>• Wind Step: 160 px / 0.15 s, invulnerable for 0.25 s.<br>• Heaven's Anvil: leap 200 × 80 px, pillars reach 160 px.<br>• Dune Quake: leap 200 × 80 px, wave reaches 240 px, stun 1.2 s (stands in for the knocked-down state of slice 6).<br><br>SP, damage and scaling are unchanged from slice 1. Each skill has 1–2 synergies with passives of its own tree. Poses and effects come from existing frames until the art lane draws their own. Shield Charge has no shield on screen until the sky-shield spell has its art. |
